Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Um, I don't know if "extremely upset" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.

I'll be right in the middle of setting up the new displays—which, by the way, those pastel cardigans I mentioned? We sold out of the lilac ones in two days. People are obsessed with purple right now. My sister just painted her bedroom this awful shade of plum, and it makes the room look so small, but she loves it. She's always had weird taste in colors though. When we were kids, she insisted on having neon green curtains. But anyway, yeah, I'll be working, and suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I can hear that huge bang, and I see the glass shattering and falling all over again, like it's happening right in that exact second. It feels so real that I actually jump sometimes. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. Um, not really distressed, no. Even when it feels super real, it's more just... jarring? Like I said, I don't freak out or anything. I just kind of freeze for a second. It's honestly just really annoying because it interrupts whatever I'm doing. 

Like if I'm ringing someone up at the register. Our registers at work are so ancient, by the way. The touch screen on mine only works if you press it really, really hard right in the top corner. The other day this lady got so mad at me because it was taking too long, and she was buying like, twenty of those little travel-sized hand sanitizers. Who needs that many? I guess maybe she was a teacher or something. Teachers don't get paid enough to deal with all those germs. My cousin teaches third grade and she's sick literally every other week. She just had to cancel our lunch plans on Sunday because she caught strep throat again. We were supposed to go to this new sushi place that opened up by the highway, the one with the half-price spicy tuna rolls on weekends. I was so bummed because I've been craving sushi for a month. 

But anyway, to answer your question... no, I wouldn't say I get distressed. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.

My sister just painted her bedroom this awful shade of plum, and it makes the room look so small, but she loves it. She's always had weird taste in colors though. When we were kids, she insisted on having neon green curtains. But anyway, yeah, I'll be working, and suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I can hear that huge bang, and I see the glass shattering and falling all over again, like it's happening right in that exact second. It feels so real that I actually jump sometimes. My coworker Sarah asked if I was okay the other day because I dropped a whole stack of folded shirts when it happened. I had to refold all of them, and those silky materials are so annoying to get straight...\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking or talking about the crash?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally came in to see you, because like I was saying earlier, I just can't make myself go near those front windows or even walk down that part of the street anymore. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. 

I actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, "I can totally do that." Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. 

But um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.

Which is super inconvenient because the bus stop I usually use is right there, and there's this coffee shop next to it that my coworker Sarah is totally obsessed with. She gets these iced matcha things every single day, and I swear they taste exactly like lawn clippings, but she loves them. I used to go with her sometimes before my shift started... but lately, I just keep finding excuses to stay in the back room at the store. Like, I'll just organize the stockroom for hours. My manager probably thinks I'm just being really proactive with the inventory, but I'm really just hiding back there, even though being out on the floor and talking to customers is supposed to be my actual job.\n\nDoctor: What happens or what do you think about when you're near the front windows at work or that part of the street?\n\nPatient: Well, it's just... the crash keeps popping into my head out of nowhere. Like, I'll be trying to set up the new spring displays, which is honestly my favorite part of the job because I get to coordinate all the colors. We just got these pastel cardigans in, and they are so cute, though they run a little small, so I have to warn people. But anyway, I'll be doing that, and suddenly it's like... I can hear that huge bang again. And I see the glass coming down, just shattering everywhere, like it's happening right then. It's so loud. My mom always said I had really sensitive hearing when I was little. I couldn't even go to fireworks shows because I'd just cry the whole time. But this is different, it's just... I can't be up there.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid the front windows at work or that part of the street because you're afraid something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think another car is gonna crash into the store. The odds of that happening twice in the exact same spot have to be super low, right? My brother is actually really into statistics, he's taking this crazy advanced math class at community college right now. He's always talking about probability and stuff, which just goes right over my head. I was never good at math. I barely passed algebra in high school, honestly. But no, it's not that I'm scared of another accident. It's just... being up there makes me feel awful. It just brings the whole thing back, like the noise and the glass and everything. So it's easier to just stay in the back where the stockroom is. Which reminds me, we got this huge shipment of summer dresses today, and they are so wrinkled from being in the boxes, I'm probably going to have to steam them all tomorrow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories of the crash that feel like you're reliving it, not just remembering it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's like... it's not just a memory, you know? Like I was saying before, it just pops into my head out of nowhere. I'll be right in the middle of setting up the new displays—which, by the way, those pastel cardigans I mentioned? We sold out of the lilac ones in two days. People are obsessed with purple right now. My sister just painted her bedroom this awful shade of plum, and it makes the room look so small, but she loves it. She's always had weird taste in colors though. When we were kids, she insisted on having neon green curtains. But anyway, yeah, I'll be working, and suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I can hear that huge bang, and I see the glass shattering and falling all over again, like it's happening right in that exact second. It feels so real that I actually jump sometimes. My coworker Sarah asked if I was okay the other day because I dropped a whole stack of folded shirts when it happened. I had to refold all of them, and those silky materials are so annoying to get straight...\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking or talking about the crash?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally came in to see you, because like I was saying earlier, I just can't make myself go near those front windows or even walk down that part of the street anymore. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Even when it feels super real, it's more just... jarring? Like I said, I don't freak out or anything. I just kind of freeze for a second. It's honestly just really annoying because it interrupts whatever I'm doing. \n\nLike if I'm ringing someone up at the register. Our registers at work are so ancient, by the way. The touch screen on mine only works if you press it really, really hard right in the top corner. The other day this lady got so mad at me because it was taking too long, and she was buying like, twenty of those little travel-sized hand sanitizers. Who needs that many? I guess maybe she was a teacher or something. Teachers don't get paid enough to deal with all those germs. My cousin teaches third grade and she's sick literally every other week. She just had to cancel our lunch plans on Sunday because she caught strep throat again. We were supposed to go to this new sushi place that opened up by the highway, the one with the half-price spicy tuna rolls on weekends. I was so bummed because I've been craving sushi for a month. \n\nBut anyway, to answer your question... no, I wouldn't say I get distressed. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nI actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, \"I can totally do that.\" Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. \n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nI actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, \"I can totally do that.\" Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. \n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any specific things, like people, places, or activities, that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 23 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Trauma_Avoidance\nmanifestation: \"I came in because I can’t make myself go near the front windows or that part of the street anymore, and I keep finding excuses to stay in the back even though that’s part of my job.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, like I just said... yeah. That's why I'm here. I just really can't go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street where it happened. I just keep making up reasons to stay in the back room with the boxes. 

Which honestly, the back room is a mess right now anyway. We got a huge shipment of holiday stuff in on Tuesday, and nobody has broken down the cardboard boxes yet. It's a huge fire hazard, honestly. My manager, Dave, is supposed to take care of the recycling, but he's been so distracted lately because his daughter just started college and she keeps calling him crying because she misses her dog. Which I totally get, because when I went to summer camp in middle school, I missed my cat so much I literally faked a stomach ache so my mom would come pick me up early. She was so mad when she figured it out, but she still took me to get ice cream on the way home. We went to this place that had the best mint chocolate chip, but they closed down a few years ago and turned into a mattress store. Why are there so many mattress stores everywhere? It feels like there's one on every corner, and they're always having a "closing sale" but they never actually close. 

But yeah, anyway, I just avoid the windows and that side of the street. I just hide out in the back even though setting up the front is supposed to be my job.

manifestation: This has been going on for about two weeks, ever since the car crashed through the store window during that overnight shift.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 23 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S070\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I don't get like, a racing heart or sweaty palms or anything when I think about it. It's just... I don't know, I just don't like thinking about it, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. 

Speaking of sweaty palms though, the air conditioning in our store has been broken for like three days. It is so miserable in there, especially in the back room where I've been hiding out. Dave keeps saying he called the repair guy, but the repair guy is always "booked up." Which I think is just an excuse because the building owner is super cheap and probably hasn't paid the last bill. He's this older guy who drives a really flashy sports car, but he won't even pay to have the leaky sink in the breakroom fixed. It just drips all day long. Drip, drip, drip. So it's easier to just stay in the back where the stockroom is. Which reminds me, we got this huge shipment of summer dresses today, and they are so wrinkled from being in the boxes, I'm probably going to have to steam them all tomorrow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories of the crash that feel like you're reliving it, not just remembering it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's like... it's not just a memory, you know? Like I was saying before, it just pops into my head out of nowhere. I'll be right in the middle of setting up the new displays—which, by the way, those pastel cardigans I mentioned? We sold out of the lilac ones in two days. People are obsessed with purple right now. My sister just painted her bedroom this awful shade of plum, and it makes the room look so small, but she loves it. She's always had weird taste in colors though. When we were kids, she insisted on having neon green curtains. But anyway, yeah, I'll be working, and suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I can hear that huge bang, and I see the glass shattering and falling all over again, like it's happening right in that exact second. It feels so real that I actually jump sometimes. My coworker Sarah asked if I was okay the other day because I dropped a whole stack of folded shirts when it happened. I had to refold all of them, and those silky materials are so annoying to get straight...\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking or talking about the crash?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally came in to see you, because like I was saying earlier, I just can't make myself go near those front windows or even walk down that part of the street anymore. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Even when it feels super real, it's more just... jarring? Like I said, I don't freak out or anything. I just kind of freeze for a second. It's honestly just really annoying because it interrupts whatever I'm doing. \n\nLike if I'm ringing someone up at the register. Our registers at work are so ancient, by the way. The touch screen on mine only works if you press it really, really hard right in the top corner. The other day this lady got so mad at me because it was taking too long, and she was buying like, twenty of those little travel-sized hand sanitizers. Who needs that many? I guess maybe she was a teacher or something. Teachers don't get paid enough to deal with all those germs. My cousin teaches third grade and she's sick literally every other week. She just had to cancel our lunch plans on Sunday because she caught strep throat again. We were supposed to go to this new sushi place that opened up by the highway, the one with the half-price spicy tuna rolls on weekends. I was so bummed because I've been craving sushi for a month. \n\nBut anyway, to answer your question... no, I wouldn't say I get distressed. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nI actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, \"I can totally do that.\" Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. \n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific things, like people, places, or activities, that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I just said... yeah. I just keep making up reasons to stay in the back room with the boxes. \n\nWhich honestly, the back room is a mess right now anyway. We got a huge shipment of holiday stuff in on Tuesday, and nobody has broken down the cardboard boxes yet. It's a huge fire hazard, honestly. My manager, Dave, is supposed to take care of the recycling, but he's been so distracted lately because his daughter just started college and she keeps calling him crying because she misses her dog. Which I totally get, because when I went to summer camp in middle school, I missed my cat so much I literally faked a stomach ache so my mom would come pick me up early. She was so mad when she figured it out, but she still took me to get ice cream on the way home. We went to this place that had the best mint chocolate chip, but they closed down a few years ago and turned into a mattress store. Why are there so many mattress stores everywhere? It feels like there's one on every corner, and they're always having a \"closing sale\" but they never actually close. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just avoid the windows and that side of the street. I just hide out in the back even though setting up the front is supposed to be my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. Which reminds me, we got this huge shipment of summer dresses today, and they are so wrinkled from being in the boxes, I'm probably going to have to steam them all tomorrow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories of the crash that feel like you're reliving it, not just remembering it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's like... it's not just a memory, you know? Like I was saying before, it just pops into my head out of nowhere. I'll be right in the middle of setting up the new displays—which, by the way, those pastel cardigans I mentioned? We sold out of the lilac ones in two days. People are obsessed with purple right now. My sister just painted her bedroom this awful shade of plum, and it makes the room look so small, but she loves it. She's always had weird taste in colors though. When we were kids, she insisted on having neon green curtains. But anyway, yeah, I'll be working, and suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I can hear that huge bang, and I see the glass shattering and falling all over again, like it's happening right in that exact second. It feels so real that I actually jump sometimes. My coworker Sarah asked if I was okay the other day because I dropped a whole stack of folded shirts when it happened. I had to refold all of them, and those silky materials are so annoying to get straight...\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking or talking about the crash?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally came in to see you, because like I was saying earlier, I just can't make myself go near those front windows or even walk down that part of the street anymore. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Even when it feels super real, it's more just... jarring? Like I said, I don't freak out or anything. I just kind of freeze for a second. It's honestly just really annoying because it interrupts whatever I'm doing. \n\nLike if I'm ringing someone up at the register. Our registers at work are so ancient, by the way. The touch screen on mine only works if you press it really, really hard right in the top corner. The other day this lady got so mad at me because it was taking too long, and she was buying like, twenty of those little travel-sized hand sanitizers. Who needs that many? I guess maybe she was a teacher or something. Teachers don't get paid enough to deal with all those germs. My cousin teaches third grade and she's sick literally every other week. She just had to cancel our lunch plans on Sunday because she caught strep throat again. We were supposed to go to this new sushi place that opened up by the highway, the one with the half-price spicy tuna rolls on weekends. I was so bummed because I've been craving sushi for a month. \n\nBut anyway, to answer your question... no, I wouldn't say I get distressed. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nI actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, \"I can totally do that.\" Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. \n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific things, like people, places, or activities, that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I just said... yeah. That's why I'm here. I just really can't go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street where it happened. I just keep making up reasons to stay in the back room with the boxes. \n\nWhich honestly, the back room is a mess right now anyway. We got a huge shipment of holiday stuff in on Tuesday, and nobody has broken down the cardboard boxes yet. It's a huge fire hazard, honestly. My manager, Dave, is supposed to take care of the recycling, but he's been so distracted lately because his daughter just started college and she keeps calling him crying because she misses her dog. Which I totally get, because when I went to summer camp in middle school, I missed my cat so much I literally faked a stomach ache so my mom would come pick me up early. She was so mad when she figured it out, but she still took me to get ice cream on the way home. We went to this place that had the best mint chocolate chip, but they closed down a few years ago and turned into a mattress store. Why are there so many mattress stores everywhere? It feels like there's one on every corner, and they're always having a \"closing sale\" but they never actually close. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just avoid the windows and that side of the street. I just hide out in the back even though setting up the front is supposed to be my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I don't get like, a racing heart or sweaty palms or anything when I think about it. It's just... I don't know, I just don't like thinking about it, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. \n\nSpeaking of sweaty palms though, the air conditioning in our store has been broken for like three days. It is so miserable in there, especially in the back room where I've been hiding out. Dave keeps saying he called the repair guy, but the repair guy is always \"booked up.\" Which I think is just an excuse because the building owner is super cheap and probably hasn't paid the last bill. He's this older guy who drives a really flashy sports car, but he won't even pay to have the leaky sink in the breakroom fixed. It just drips all day long. Drip, drip, drip. It drives me crazy when I'm trying to eat my lunch. I usually just bring a sandwich, but lately I've been super into those little frozen burritos. You know the ones? You microwave them for a minute and a half, but the middle is always still freezing cold, so you have to put it back in, and then the beans explode everywhere. It's a whole process. \n\nBut um, yeah, what were you asking? Oh, anxious. No, I don't really feel anxious about it. Just, you know, it is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides the front windows and that part of the street that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I mean, like I keep saying... it's just the front windows and that part of the street. That's literally the whole reason I'm here. I just can't go near them, and I keep finding excuses to stay in the back room even though setting up the front is my job. \n\nThough honestly, the back room is getting kind of claustrophobic anyway. We have all these boxes stacked up to the ceiling, and I swear one of them is going to fall on me one day. I was actually watching this documentary the other night about people who hoard things, and their houses just look like our back room. It's so sad, but also kind of fascinating? Like, how do you get to the point where you keep every single newspaper from 1995? My grandma used to save all those little plastic bread tags. She had a whole coffee can full of them. She said she was going to use them to label the cords behind the TV, but she never did. She just kept collecting them. She also collected those little porcelain thimbles from different states. I think she had like, all fifty of them. \n\nBut um, yeah, no other things I'm avoiding. Just the windows and the street. I just stay in the back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, outside my body? Yeah, actually. Sometimes when I have to go out on the sales floor, everything just feels... weird. Like the fluorescent lights and the clothing racks all seem really far away and fake, almost. And I feel like I'm just watching myself move through the store instead of actually being the one walking. It's super trippy. 

Kind of like when you go to the movie theater during the day, and then you walk outside after the movie and the sun is so bright it just completely disorients you? I hate that feeling. I went to see that new superhero movie last weekend with my friend Sarah, the one where the guy has the cape that turns into a shield. The CGI was terrible, honestly. You could totally tell when they were using a green screen because the lighting on their faces didn't match the background. Sarah didn't even notice, but she's always on her phone during movies anyway. She’s one of those people who turns their brightness all the way down and thinks nobody can see them texting, but it's like, we can all see the glow, Sarah. She was texting this guy she met on a dating app, but his profile picture was him holding a fish, which is always a red flag, right? Like, why do guys always post pictures of fish? 

But yeah, it's just this weird feeling on the floor, like I'm watching a movie of myself walking around the store, and everything is just... fake.

My coworker Sarah asked if I was okay the other day because I dropped a whole stack of folded shirts when it happened. I had to refold all of them, and those silky materials are so annoying to get straight...\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking or talking about the crash?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally came in to see you, because like I was saying earlier, I just can't make myself go near those front windows or even walk down that part of the street anymore. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Even when it feels super real, it's more just... jarring? Like I said, I don't freak out or anything. I just kind of freeze for a second. It's honestly just really annoying because it interrupts whatever I'm doing. \n\nLike if I'm ringing someone up at the register. Our registers at work are so ancient, by the way. The touch screen on mine only works if you press it really, really hard right in the top corner. The other day this lady got so mad at me because it was taking too long, and she was buying like, twenty of those little travel-sized hand sanitizers. Who needs that many? I guess maybe she was a teacher or something. Teachers don't get paid enough to deal with all those germs. My cousin teaches third grade and she's sick literally every other week. She just had to cancel our lunch plans on Sunday because she caught strep throat again. We were supposed to go to this new sushi place that opened up by the highway, the one with the half-price spicy tuna rolls on weekends. I was so bummed because I've been craving sushi for a month. \n\nBut anyway, to answer your question... no, I wouldn't say I get distressed. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nI actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, \"I can totally do that.\" Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. \n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific things, like people, places, or activities, that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I just said... yeah. That's why I'm here. I just really can't go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street where it happened. I just keep making up reasons to stay in the back room with the boxes. \n\nWhich honestly, the back room is a mess right now anyway. We got a huge shipment of holiday stuff in on Tuesday, and nobody has broken down the cardboard boxes yet. It's a huge fire hazard, honestly. My manager, Dave, is supposed to take care of the recycling, but he's been so distracted lately because his daughter just started college and she keeps calling him crying because she misses her dog. Which I totally get, because when I went to summer camp in middle school, I missed my cat so much I literally faked a stomach ache so my mom would come pick me up early. She was so mad when she figured it out, but she still took me to get ice cream on the way home. We went to this place that had the best mint chocolate chip, but they closed down a few years ago and turned into a mattress store. Why are there so many mattress stores everywhere? It feels like there's one on every corner, and they're always having a \"closing sale\" but they never actually close. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just avoid the windows and that side of the street. I just hide out in the back even though setting up the front is supposed to be my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I don't get like, a racing heart or sweaty palms or anything when I think about it. It's just... I don't know, I just don't like thinking about it, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. \n\nSpeaking of sweaty palms though, the air conditioning in our store has been broken for like three days. It is so miserable in there, especially in the back room where I've been hiding out. Dave keeps saying he called the repair guy, but the repair guy is always \"booked up.\" Which I think is just an excuse because the building owner is super cheap and probably hasn't paid the last bill. He's this older guy who drives a really flashy sports car, but he won't even pay to have the leaky sink in the breakroom fixed. It just drips all day long. Drip, drip, drip. It drives me crazy when I'm trying to eat my lunch. I usually just bring a sandwich, but lately I've been super into those little frozen burritos. You know the ones? You microwave them for a minute and a half, but the middle is always still freezing cold, so you have to put it back in, and then the beans explode everywhere. It's a whole process. \n\nBut um, yeah, what were you asking? Oh, anxious. No, I don't really feel anxious about it. Just, you know, it is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides the front windows and that part of the street that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I mean, like I keep saying... it's just the front windows and that part of the street. That's literally the whole reason I'm here. I just can't go near them, and I keep finding excuses to stay in the back room even though setting up the front is my job. \n\nThough honestly, the back room is getting kind of claustrophobic anyway. We have all these boxes stacked up to the ceiling, and I swear one of them is going to fall on me one day. I was actually watching this documentary the other night about people who hoard things, and their houses just look like our back room. It's so sad, but also kind of fascinating? Like, how do you get to the point where you keep every single newspaper from 1995? My grandma used to save all those little plastic bread tags. She had a whole coffee can full of them. She said she was going to use them to label the cords behind the TV, but she never did. She just kept collecting them. She also collected those little porcelain thimbles from different states. I think she had like, all fifty of them. \n\nBut um, yeah, no other things I'm avoiding. Just the windows and the street. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body or watching yourself when you have these memories of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, outside my body? Yeah, actually. Sometimes when I have to go out on the sales floor, everything just feels... weird. Like the fluorescent lights and the clothing racks all seem really far away and fake, almost. And I feel like I'm just watching myself move through the store instead of actually being the one walking. It's super trippy. \n\nKind of like when you go to the movie theater during the day, and then you walk outside after the movie and the sun is so bright it just completely disorients you? I hate that feeling. I went to see that new superhero movie last weekend with my friend Sarah, the one where the guy has the cape that turns into a shield. The CGI was terrible, honestly. Um, spaced out? No, I don't really think so. Not unless I'm having one of those weird moments out on the floor. Otherwise, I feel pretty normal, just, you know, hanging out in the back room.

I can hear that huge bang again. And I see the glass coming down, just shattering everywhere, like it's happening right then. It's so loud. My mom always said I had really sensitive hearing when I was little. I couldn't even go to fireworks shows because I'd just cry the whole time. But this is different, it's just... I can't be up there.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid the front windows at work or that part of the street because you're afraid something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think another car is gonna crash into the store. The odds of that happening twice in the exact same spot have to be super low, right? My brother is actually really into statistics, he's taking this crazy advanced math class at community college right now. He's always talking about probability and stuff, which just goes right over my head. I was never good at math. I barely passed algebra in high school, honestly. But no, it's not that I'm scared of another accident. It's just... being up there makes me feel awful. It just brings the whole thing back, like the noise and the glass and everything. So it's easier to just stay in the back where the stockroom is. Which reminds me, we got this huge shipment of summer dresses today, and they are so wrinkled from being in the boxes, I'm probably going to have to steam them all tomorrow...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories of the crash that feel like you're reliving it, not just remembering it?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's like... it's not just a memory, you know? Like I was saying before, it just pops into my head out of nowhere. I'll be right in the middle of setting up the new displays—which, by the way, those pastel cardigans I mentioned? We sold out of the lilac ones in two days. People are obsessed with purple right now. My sister just painted her bedroom this awful shade of plum, and it makes the room look so small, but she loves it. She's always had weird taste in colors though. When we were kids, she insisted on having neon green curtains. But anyway, yeah, I'll be working, and suddenly it's like I'm right back there. I can hear that huge bang, and I see the glass shattering and falling all over again, like it's happening right in that exact second. It feels so real that I actually jump sometimes. My coworker Sarah asked if I was okay the other day because I dropped a whole stack of folded shirts when it happened. I had to refold all of them, and those silky materials are so annoying to get straight...\n\nDoctor: Do you try to avoid thinking or talking about the crash?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I really try not to. If anyone brings it up, I just kind of change the subject. Honestly, that's the main reason I finally came in to see you, because like I was saying earlier, I just can't make myself go near those front windows or even walk down that part of the street anymore. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. She's usually pretty chill, she actually used to manage this really high-end shoe store at the mall before it closed down. Malls are just so depressing now, aren't they? I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. I mean, I guess it bothers me? But it's not like I'm breaking down crying or anything. It's more just... uncomfortable. Speaking of uncomfortable, the new uniform shirts they made us start wearing are so itchy. They're this weird synthetic blend, and I swear I'm allergic to it or something because my neck gets all red right where the collar hits. I was telling my friend Jessica about it—she works at the coffee shop next door, the one that makes those amazing iced matcha lattes. I usually get one every morning before my shift, but they raised their prices again last week, which is just ridiculous. Like, six dollars for a drink? That's almost an hour of my pay after taxes. It's so hard to save money right now. I've been trying to put away a little bit each week so I can eventually get a new car, because my Honda is making this terrible rattling noise when I go over 40. But yeah, the memories... I just don't really like it when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel really distressed when those memories of the crash feel like they're happening all over again?\n\nPatient: Um, not really distressed, no. Even when it feels super real, it's more just... jarring? Like I said, I don't freak out or anything. I just kind of freeze for a second. It's honestly just really annoying because it interrupts whatever I'm doing. \n\nLike if I'm ringing someone up at the register. Our registers at work are so ancient, by the way. The touch screen on mine only works if you press it really, really hard right in the top corner. The other day this lady got so mad at me because it was taking too long, and she was buying like, twenty of those little travel-sized hand sanitizers. Who needs that many? I guess maybe she was a teacher or something. Teachers don't get paid enough to deal with all those germs. My cousin teaches third grade and she's sick literally every other week. She just had to cancel our lunch plans on Sunday because she caught strep throat again. We were supposed to go to this new sushi place that opened up by the highway, the one with the half-price spicy tuna rolls on weekends. I was so bummed because I've been craving sushi for a month. \n\nBut anyway, to answer your question... no, I wouldn't say I get distressed. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. I just... I can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room with the inventory. Which is bad, because being out on the floor and setting up the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nI actually used to really love doing the displays. Last month I did this whole autumn theme with these fake leaves and little pumpkins that I found at the craft store. Craft stores are so dangerous for me, honestly. I go in for one thing and come out with fifty dollars worth of stuff I don't need. Like last time, I bought all this yarn because I decided I was going to learn how to crochet. I saw this girl on TikTok making these adorable little stuffed frogs, and I was like, \"I can totally do that.\" Spoiler alert: I cannot do that. I watched like ten tutorials and my frog just looked like a green blob. My cat ended up stealing it and now it's under the couch somewhere. He loves batting things under the couch, especially my hair ties. I swear I buy a new pack of hair ties every month and they just vanish. \n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding the front windows and that whole side of the street entirely. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific things, like people, places, or activities, that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I just said... yeah. That's why I'm here. I just really can't go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even walk down that part of the street where it happened. I just keep making up reasons to stay in the back room with the boxes. \n\nWhich honestly, the back room is a mess right now anyway. We got a huge shipment of holiday stuff in on Tuesday, and nobody has broken down the cardboard boxes yet. It's a huge fire hazard, honestly. My manager, Dave, is supposed to take care of the recycling, but he's been so distracted lately because his daughter just started college and she keeps calling him crying because she misses her dog. Which I totally get, because when I went to summer camp in middle school, I missed my cat so much I literally faked a stomach ache so my mom would come pick me up early. She was so mad when she figured it out, but she still took me to get ice cream on the way home. We went to this place that had the best mint chocolate chip, but they closed down a few years ago and turned into a mattress store. Why are there so many mattress stores everywhere? It feels like there's one on every corner, and they're always having a \"closing sale\" but they never actually close. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just avoid the windows and that side of the street. I just hide out in the back even though setting up the front is supposed to be my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I don't get like, a racing heart or sweaty palms or anything when I think about it. It's just... I don't know, I just don't like thinking about it, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. \n\nSpeaking of sweaty palms though, the air conditioning in our store has been broken for like three days. It is so miserable in there, especially in the back room where I've been hiding out. Dave keeps saying he called the repair guy, but the repair guy is always \"booked up.\" Which I think is just an excuse because the building owner is super cheap and probably hasn't paid the last bill. He's this older guy who drives a really flashy sports car, but he won't even pay to have the leaky sink in the breakroom fixed. It just drips all day long. Drip, drip, drip. It drives me crazy when I'm trying to eat my lunch. I usually just bring a sandwich, but lately I've been super into those little frozen burritos. You know the ones? You microwave them for a minute and a half, but the middle is always still freezing cold, so you have to put it back in, and then the beans explode everywhere. It's a whole process. \n\nBut um, yeah, what were you asking? Oh, anxious. No, I don't really feel anxious about it. Just, you know, it is what it is.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things besides the front windows and that part of the street that you avoid because they remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I mean, like I keep saying... it's just the front windows and that part of the street. That's literally the whole reason I'm here. I just can't go near them, and I keep finding excuses to stay in the back room even though setting up the front is my job. \n\nThough honestly, the back room is getting kind of claustrophobic anyway. We have all these boxes stacked up to the ceiling, and I swear one of them is going to fall on me one day. I was actually watching this documentary the other night about people who hoard things, and their houses just look like our back room. It's so sad, but also kind of fascinating? Like, how do you get to the point where you keep every single newspaper from 1995? My grandma used to save all those little plastic bread tags. She had a whole coffee can full of them. She said she was going to use them to label the cords behind the TV, but she never did. She just kept collecting them. She also collected those little porcelain thimbles from different states. I think she had like, all fifty of them. \n\nBut um, yeah, no other things I'm avoiding. Just the windows and the street. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body or watching yourself when you have these memories of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, outside my body? Yeah, actually. Sometimes when I have to go out on the sales floor, everything just feels... weird. Like the fluorescent lights and the clothing racks all seem really far away and fake, almost. And I feel like I'm just watching myself move through the store instead of actually being the one walking. It's super trippy. \n\nKind of like when you go to the movie theater during the day, and then you walk outside after the movie and the sun is so bright it just completely disorients you? I hate that feeling. I went to see that new superhero movie last weekend with my friend Sarah, the one where the guy has the cape that turns into a shield. The CGI was terrible, honestly. You could totally tell when they were using a green screen because the lighting on their faces didn't match the background. Sarah didn't even notice, but she's always on her phone during movies anyway. She’s one of those people who turns their brightness all the way down and thinks nobody can see them texting, but it's like, we can all see the glow, Sarah. She was texting this guy she met on a dating app, but his profile picture was him holding a fish, which is always a red flag, right? Like, why do guys always post pictures of fish? \n\nBut yeah, it's just this weird feeling on the floor, like I'm watching a movie of myself walking around the store, and everything is just... fake.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a daze or spaced out when you're not having memories of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, spaced out? No, I don't really think so. Not unless I'm having one of those weird moments out on the floor. Otherwise, I feel pretty normal, just, you know, hanging out in the back room. \n\nWhich reminds me, I really need to clean out my locker back there. I think I left a banana in there before my days off, and I'm terrified to open it now. Have you ever smelled a rotten banana in a confined space? It's the worst. My brother used to hide banana peels under my bed when we were kids, just to be annoying. He was always doing stuff like that. One time he put a plastic spider in my cereal box. I poured it out and screamed so loud my mom dropped a whole carton of eggs on the kitchen floor. It took forever to clean up, and the dog kept trying to eat the raw eggs, which is supposed to be good for their coats, right? But my mom was freaking out about salmonella. She’s kind of a germaphobe. She makes everyone take their shoes off at the front door and puts them in this little cubby thing, but the cubby is always full of my dad's gross work boots. \n\nBut um, no, I don't feel in a daze or anything normally. Just when I get that weird movie feeling.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

She said she was going to use them to label the cords behind the TV, but she never did. She just kept collecting them. She also collected those little porcelain thimbles from different states. I think she had like, all fifty of them. \n\nBut um, yeah, no other things I'm avoiding. Just the windows and the street. I just stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're outside of your body or watching yourself when you have these memories of the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, outside my body? Yeah, actually. Sometimes when I have to go out on the sales floor, everything just feels... weird. Like the fluorescent lights and the clothing racks all seem really far away and fake, almost. And I feel like I'm just watching myself move through the store instead of actually being the one walking. It's super trippy. \n\nKind of like when you go to the movie theater during the day, and then you walk outside after the movie and the sun is so bright it just completely disorients you? I hate that feeling. I went to see that new superhero movie last weekend with my friend Sarah, the one where the guy has the cape that turns into a shield. The CGI was terrible, honestly. You could totally tell when they were using a green screen because the lighting on their faces didn't match the background. Sarah didn't even notice, but she's always on her phone during movies anyway. She’s one of those people who turns their brightness all the way down and thinks nobody can see them texting, but it's like, we can all see the glow, Sarah. I think I left a banana in there before my days off, and I'm terrified to open it now. Have you ever smelled a rotten banana in a confined space? It's the worst. My brother used to hide banana peels under my bed when we were kids, just to be annoying. He was always doing stuff like that. One time he put a plastic spider in my cereal box. I poured it out and screamed so loud my mom dropped a whole carton of eggs on the kitchen floor. It took forever to clean up, and the dog kept trying to eat the raw eggs, which is supposed to be good for their coats, right? But my mom was freaking out about salmonella. She’s kind of a germaphobe. Like, I'll be trying to set up the new spring displays, which is honestly my favorite part of the job because I get to coordinate all the colors. We just got these pastel cardigans in, and they are so cute, though they run a little small, so I have to warn people. But anyway, I'll be doing that, and suddenly it's like... I can hear that huge bang again. And I see the glass coming down, just shattering everywhere, like it's happening right then. It's so loud. My mom always said I had really sensitive hearing when I was little. I couldn't even go to fireworks shows because I'd just cry the whole time. But this is different, it's just... I can't be up there.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid the front windows at work or that part of the street because you're afraid something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think another car is gonna crash into the store. The odds of that happening twice in the exact same spot have to be super low, right? My brother is actually really into statistics, he's taking this crazy advanced math class at community college right now. He's always talking about probability and stuff, which just goes right over my head. I was never good at math. I barely passed algebra in high school, honestly. So it's easier to just stay in the back where the stockroom is. I just keep finding all these excuses to stay in the back room, even though being out on the floor and doing the displays is a huge part of my job. \n\nMy manager Brenda is definitely starting to notice, I think. I went to the one by my apartment last weekend just because I was craving a pretzel—the cinnamon sugar ones are literally the best, I always get extra icing—and it was basically a ghost town in there. Half the food court was just completely boarded up, which is sad because I used to hang out there all the time in high school. It's crazy how much things change. But um, yeah, I just try to hide in the back with the boxes so I don't have to think about the accident or talk to anyone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel extremely upset when these memories of the crash come back to you?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if \"extremely upset\" is the right word. Just kind of startled for a minute, and then I just want to get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid places, people, or activities that remind you of the crash?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was saying earlier, that's actually the main reason I finally made the appointment to come see you. It feels like there's one on every corner, and they're always having a \"closing sale\" but they never actually close. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just avoid the windows and that side of the street. I just hide out in the back even though setting up the front is supposed to be my job.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you think about the crash?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I don't know, I just don't like thinking about it, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. \n\nSpeaking of sweaty palms though, the air conditioning in our store has been broken for like three days. It is so miserable in there, especially in the back room where I've been hiding out. Dave keeps saying he called the repair guy, but the repair guy is always \"booked up.\" Which I think is just an excuse because the building owner is super cheap and probably hasn't paid the last bill. He's this older guy who drives a really flashy sports car, but he won't even pay to have the leaky sink in the breakroom fixed. It just drips all day long. Drip, drip, drip. My grandpa used to play golf, but he gave it up after he hit a goose with a golf ball by accident. He felt so bad he joined a bird-watching society and bought all these expensive binoculars. He used to sit on his back porch for hours just watching the bird feeder. The squirrels kept stealing the birdseed, though, so he greased the pole with Vaseline. It was hilarious watching them try to climb up and just sliding right back down. \n\nBut yeah, like I said, it just happens on the floor sometimes. I just feel like I'm watching myself from the outside.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other reminders of the crash, such as certain smells or sounds, that you try to avoid?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually, that's kind of the main reason I came in. I just can't make myself go near the front windows of the store anymore, or even that part of the street where it happened. I keep finding all these excuses to just stay in the back room, even though working the floor is, like, my actual job. \n\nMy manager is getting super annoyed with me because I keep volunteering to do inventory instead. Inventory is usually the absolute worst. We have these little scanner guns that are supposed to make it easy, but the batteries are always dying, and half the time they don't even read the barcodes right. You have to manually type in this twelve-digit SKU number, and if you mess up one number, it logs it as, like, a pair of men's cargo shorts instead of a floral blouse. My coworker Dave got in so much trouble last month because he accidentally scanned a whole rack of winter coats as summer swimwear. The regional manager had to come down and do an audit, and he is this super intense guy who wears these really loud patterned ties. Like, one day he wore a tie with tiny little hotdogs all over it. Who wears a hotdog tie to a corporate audit? It made it really hard to take him seriously when he was yelling at Dave about the swimwear discrepancy. Dave ended up quitting anyway to go work at a smoothie place in the mall. He says he gets free smoothies, but honestly, how much kale and spinach can one person drink? \n\nBut yeah, I just...
